{"parentUuid":"164dc5e8-6471-49de-9925-25779a60db3b","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"2729f8f9-d8fc-49c4-8724-96c854c33c88","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01FRCbDQjWGjkcpxkavLQG3a","type":"tool_result","content":"## Part 4 — Exercise A: the context-threshold hook\n\n**Time: 30 min.** Full instructions: `docs/mp2-setup_context-threshold-hook_20260922.md` in your repo. Summary:\n\nYou will finish a `Stop` hook that warns you when your context window crosses 50%, 60%, and 80%, and a `PreCompact` hook that logs every compaction. The status line shows a live meter.\n\nThe rule the meter serves: **`/clear` between short tickets; `/compact` at a green test for long ones**, always with a focus note like\n\n```text\n/compact keep: ticket #5 acceptance criteria, what is left, files touched\n```\n\nYour job is `decide()` in `.claude/hooks/ctx_guard.py`; seven tests fail until it works:\n\n```text\n/clear\n/tdd Implement decide() in .claude/hooks/ctx_guard.py so the failing tests in tests/test_ctx_guard.py pass. Do not change the tests.\n```\n\nGraded as B6.\n\n---\n\n----\n#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"Stop hook: warn when the context window crosses 50% / 60% / 80%.\n\nClaude Code runs this after every Claude turn (the Stop event) and pipes the\nevent JSON to stdin. It reads the percentage the status line recorded, and\nprints {\"systemMessage\": \"...\"} the first time usage crosses a threshold.\nThe message is shown to YOU, not to Claude. The hook never blocks and never\nruns /compact itself: slash commands are yours to type.\n\nThresholds (override when launching:  CTX_WARN_PCT=1 claude  to test):\n  CTX_WARN_PCT     default 50  -> \"plan a /compact\"\n  CTX_COMPACT_PCT  default 60  -> \"/compact at the next green test\"\n  urgent = CTX_COMPACT_PCT + 20 -> \"compact or /handoff now; consider splitting\"\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport csv\nimport json\nimport os\nimport sys\nfrom datetime import datetime, timezone\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nNONE, WARN, COMPACT, URGENT = 0, 1, 2, 3\n\n\ndef thresholds() -> tuple[float, float, float]:\n    warn = float(os.environ.get(\"CTX_WARN_PCT\", 50))\n    compact = float(os.environ.get(\"CTX_COMPACT_PCT\", 60))\n    return warn, compact, compact + 20\n\n\ndef band_for(pct: float, warn: float, compact: float, urgent: float) -> int:\n    if pct >= urgent:\n        return URGENT\n    if pct >= compact:\n        return COMPACT\n    if pct >= warn:\n        return WARN\n    return NONE\n\n\ndef message_for(band: int, pct: float) -> str:\n    p = f\"{pct:.0f}%\"\n    return {\n        WARN: (\n            f\"Context {p}: finish the current red-green cycle, then plan a \"\n            \"/compact at the next green test.\"\n        ),\n        COMPACT: (\n            f\"Context {p}: at the next green test run  /compact keep: ticket #N \"\n            \"acceptance criteria, what is left, files touched\"\n        ),\n        URGENT: (\n            f\"Context {p}: compact now (or /handoff and start fresh). If this \"\n            \"ticket keeps overflowing, split it with /to-tickets.\"\n        ),\n    }[band]\n\n\ndef decide(pct: float, last_band: int) -> tuple[str | None, int]:\n    \"\"\"Pure function (unit-tested): return (message or None, band to store).\n\n    Warn once per upward crossing. When usage drops (after /compact or\n    /clear), store the lower band so the next crossing warns again.\n    \"\"\"\n    band = band_for(pct, *thresholds())\n    if band > last_band:\n        return message_for(band, pct), band\n    return None, band\n\nLEDGER_COLUMNS = [\n    \"ts\", \"session\", \"phase\", \"provider\", \"model\", \"effort\",\n    \"ctx_pct\", \"five_h_pct\", \"weekly_pct\",\n]\n\n\ndef log_usage(project: Path, session: str, state: dict) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Append one row per turn to notes/usage-ledger.csv (rubric item B7).\"\"\"\n    ledger = project / \"notes\" / \"usage-ledger.csv\"\n    phase_file = project / \".claude\" / \"state\" / \"phase\"\n    try:\n        phase = phase_file.read_text().strip() or \"unlabelled\"\n    except OSError:\n        phase = \"unlabelled\"\n\n    def cell(value: object) -> str:\n        return \"\" if value is None else str(value)\n\n    row = [\n        datetime.now(timezone.utc).strftime(\"%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ\"),\n        session[:8], phase, cell(state.get(\"provider\")), cell(state.get(\"model\")),\n        cell(state.get(\"effort\")), f\"{float(state.get('pct', 0)):.1f}\",\n        cell(state.get(\"five_h\")), cell(state.get(\"weekly\")),\n    ]\n    try:\n        ledger.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n        new = not ledger.exists()\n        with ledger.open(\"a\", newline=\"\") as f:\n            writer = csv.writer(f)\n            if new:\n                writer.writerow(LEDGER_COLUMNS)\n            writer.writerow(row)\n    except OSError:\n        pass\n\n\ndef main() -> None:\n    try:\n        event = json.load(sys.stdin)\n    except Exception:\n        return\n    project = Path(os.environ.get(\"CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR\") or event.get(\"cwd\") or os.getcwd())\n    session = event.get(\"session_id\") or \"default\"\n    state_dir = project / \".claude\" / \"state\"\n last_band)\n    try:\n        band_file.write_text(json.dumps({\"band\": band}))\n    except OSError:\n        pass\n    if msg:\n        print(json.dumps({\"systemMessage\": msg}))\n\n\nif __name__ The judge checks that the hook works and reads `docs/process/compaction-log.md`.\n**Verified against:** Claude Code hooks and status-line docs as of 2026-09-22. Record your own version with `claude --version` in `notes/setup_check.md`.\n\n---\n\n## Why you are building this\n\nPocock's loop runs one ticket per fresh context: `/clear`, then `/implement #N`. Most tickets finish well inside one window. A long ticket does not, and a model working in a very full context forgets the ticket's acceptance criteria and starts to drift from the spec. The course rule is:\n\n- **Short ticket:** `/clear` before it. Nothing else.\n- **Long ticket:** once context passes **50–60%**, `/compact` at the next **green test** (the end of a `/tdd` red-green-refactor cycle), never in the middle of a failing test, and always with a focus note.\n\nYou cannot follow that rule if you cannot see the number. This exercise gives you a live meter and a warning that fires at the right moment.\n\n## How it works\n\n```mermaid\nflowchart LR\n    CC[\"Claude Code session\"] -- \"session JSON on stdin<br/>(every refresh)\" --> SL[\"ctx_statusline.py<br/>prints: [Opus] ctx 47% ▓▓▓▓▓░░░░░\"]\n    SL -- \"writes\" --> ST[\".claude/state/ctx_&lt;session&gt;.json<br/>{pct: 47.2}\"]\n    CC -- \"Stop event<br/>(end of every Claude turn)\" --> G[\"ctx_guard.py\"]\n    ST -- \"reads\" --> G\n    G -- \"first time over 50 / 60 / 80%\" --> W[\"⚠ systemMessage shown to YOU\"]\n    CC -- \"PreCompact event\" --> L[\"ctx_compact_log.py\"]\n    L -- \"appends\" --> LOG[\"docs/process/compaction-log.md<br/>(grading evidence)\"]\n```\n\nThree facts from the Claude Code docs make this design work:\n\n1. **The status line already receives the context percentage** as `context_window.used_percentage` on stdin. The status line is the customizable bar under your prompt.\n2. **A `Stop` hook fires every time Claude finishes a turn.** The end of a turn is where a natural boundary (a green test) happens, so that is when the warning should appear.\n3. **A hook's `systemMessage` is shown to the user, not to Claude.** The warning talks to you, and does not pollute Claude's context.\n\nA hook cannot type `/compact` for you. Slash commands are yours. The hook only warns; you decide.\n\nEach herdr pane or terminal tab running Claude has its own `session_id`, so parallel sessions keep separate meters and never trip each other's warnings.\n\n## The four files\n\n| File | Job | Runs when |\n|---|---|---|\n| `.claude/hooks/ctx_statusline.py` | Shows the meter; records the percentage | Every status-line refresh |\n| `.claude/hooks/ctx_guard.py` | Warns at 50 / 60 / 80% | End of every Claude turn (`Stop`) |\n| `.claude/hooks/ctx_compact_log.py` | Logs each compaction and its focus note | Before every compaction (`PreCompact`) |\n| `tests/test_ctx_guard.py` | Proves the logic works | When `/tdd` or `/code-review` runs the suite |\n\n---\n\n## Step 1 — Check Python\n\nIn Ghostty (macOS) or Windows Terminal (WSL2), inside your `iphs400-mp2-cms` folder:\n\n```bash\npython3 --version\n```","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"d3256ace-a770-4188-b537-4a4433c67c01","timestamp":"2026-10-13T00:54:07.462Z","toolUseResult":{"stdout":"## Part 4 — Exercise A: the context-threshold hook\n\n**Time: 30 min.** Full instructions: `docs/mp2-setup_context-threshold-hook_20260922.md` in your repo. Summary:\n\nYou will finish a `Stop` hook that warns you when your context window crosses 50%, 60%, and 80%, and a `PreCompact` hook that logs every compaction. The status line shows a live meter.\n\nThe rule the meter serves: **`/clear` between short tickets; `/compact` at a green test for long ones**, always with a focus note like\n\n```text\n/compact keep: ticket #5 acceptance criteria, what is left, files touched\n```\n\nYour job is `decide()` in `.claude/hooks/ctx_guard.py`; seven tests fail until it works:\n\n```text\n/clear\n/tdd Implement decide() in .claude/hooks/ctx_guard.py so the failing tests in tests/test_ctx_guard.py pass. Do not change the tests.\n```\n\nGraded as B6.\n\n---\n\n----\n#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"Stop hook: warn when the context window crosses 50% / 60% / 80%.\n\nClaude Code runs this after every Claude turn (the Stop event) and pipes the\nevent JSON to stdin. It reads the percentage the status line recorded, and\nprints {\"systemMessage\": \"...\"} the first time usage crosses a threshold.\nThe message is shown to YOU, not to Claude. The hook never blocks and never\nruns /compact itself: slash commands are yours to type.\n\nThresholds (override when launching:  CTX_WARN_PCT=1 claude  to test):\n  CTX_WARN_PCT     default 50  -> \"plan a /compact\"\n  CTX_COMPACT_PCT  default 60  -> \"/compact at the next green test\"\n  urgent = CTX_COMPACT_PCT + 20 -> \"compact or /handoff now; consider splitting\"\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport csv\nimport json\nimport os\nimport sys\nfrom datetime import datetime, timezone\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nNONE, WARN, COMPACT, URGENT = 0, 1, 2, 3\n\n\ndef thresholds() -> tuple[float, float, float]:\n    warn = float(os.environ.get(\"CTX_WARN_PCT\", 50))\n    compact = float(os.environ.get(\"CTX_COMPACT_PCT\", 60))\n    return warn, compact, compact + 20\n\n\ndef band_for(pct: float, warn: float, compact: float, urgent: float) -> int:\n    if pct >= urgent:\n        return URGENT\n    if pct >= compact:\n        return COMPACT\n    if pct >= warn:\n        return WARN\n    return NONE\n\n\ndef message_for(band: int, pct: float) -> str:\n    p = f\"{pct:.0f}%\"\n    return {\n        WARN: (\n            f\"Context {p}: finish the current red-green cycle, then plan a \"\n            \"/compact at the next green test.\"\n        ),\n        COMPACT: (\n            f\"Context {p}: at the next green test run  /compact keep: ticket #N \"\n            \"acceptance criteria, what is left, files touched\"\n        ),\n        URGENT: (\n            f\"Context {p}: compact now (or /handoff and start fresh). If this \"\n            \"ticket keeps overflowing, split it with /to-tickets.\"\n        ),\n    }[band]\n\n\ndef decide(pct: float, last_band: int) -> tuple[str | None, int]:\n    \"\"\"Pure function (unit-tested): return (message or None, band to store).\n\n    Warn once per upward crossing. When usage drops (after /compact or\n    /clear), store the lower band so the next crossing warns again.\n    \"\"\"\n    band = band_for(pct, *thresholds())\n    if band > last_band:\n        return message_for(band, pct), band\n    return None, band\n\nLEDGER_COLUMNS = [\n    \"ts\", \"session\", \"phase\", \"provider\", \"model\", \"effort\",\n    \"ctx_pct\", \"five_h_pct\", \"weekly_pct\",\n]\n\n\ndef log_usage(project: Path, session: str, state: dict) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Append one row per turn to notes/usage-ledger.csv (rubric item B7).\"\"\"\n    ledger = project / \"notes\" / \"usage-ledger.csv\"\n    phase_file = project / \".claude\" / \"state\" / \"phase\"\n    try:\n        phase = phase_file.read_text().strip() or \"unlabelled\"\n    except OSError:\n        phase = \"unlabelled\"\n\n    def cell(value: object) -> str:\n        return \"\" if value is None else str(value)\n\n    row = [\n        datetime.now(timezone.utc).strftime(\"%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ\"),\n        session[:8], phase, cell(state.get(\"provider\")), cell(state.get(\"model\")),\n        cell(state.get(\"effort\")), f\"{float(state.get('pct', 0)):.1f}\",\n        cell(state.get(\"five_h\")), cell(state.get(\"weekly\")),\n    ]\n    try:\n        ledger.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n        new = not ledger.exists()\n        with ledger.open(\"a\", newline=\"\") as f:\n            writer = csv.writer(f)\n            if new:\n                writer.writerow(LEDGER_COLUMNS)\n            writer.writerow(row)\n    except OSError:\n        pass\n\n\ndef main() -> None:\n    try:\n        event = json.load(sys.stdin)\n    except Exception:\n        return\n    project = Path(os.environ.get(\"CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR\") or event.get(\"cwd\") or os.getcwd())\n    session = event.get(\"session_id\") or \"default\"\n    state_dir = project / \".claude\" / \"state\"\n    ctx_file = state_dir / f\"ctx_{session}.json\"\n    band_file = state_dir / f\"ctx_{session}_band.json\"\n\n    try:\n        state = json.loads(ctx_file.read_text())\n        pct = float(state[\"pct\"])\n    except (OSError, ValueError, KeyError):\n        return  # no status-line data yet: stay silent\n    try:\n        last_band = int(json.loads(band_file.read_text())[\"band\"])\n    except (OSError, ValueError, KeyError):\n        last_band = NONE\n\n    log_usage(project, session, state)\n\n    msg, band = decide(pct, echo ----; cat notes/token-budget-plan.md; echo ----; git diff scripts/usage_report.py","description":"Read Part 5, budget plan, report diff"}},"wireIngestContext":{"toolu_01KUe5SnvVt845K4bgxenRE1":{"cwd":"/home/joe/code/iphs400-mp2-cms"}},"serverClassifierRequest":"76e4b4a4-4aa2-457c-885f-f14ad28a087b","apiBlockIndex":0,"requestId":"req_011CfyKwfMHBjgmsVFUUkevh","type":"assistant","uuid":"e067c467-c6c2-4d68-aa7d-7bc3e5db4ced","timestamp":"2026-10-13T00:57:07.400Z","effort":"medium","perTurnEffort":"medium","session_id":"86c96d58-6503-4970-83eb-16ca927e0436","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/joe/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"ba22b36e-dd9d-481b-8941-dd077ffc3f61","version":"2.1.284","gitBranch":"main"}
{"parentUuid":"e067c467-c6c2-4d68-aa7d-7bc3e5db4ced","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"7e40fdac-e542-4414-8f0e-54c51db82124","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01KUe5SnvVt845K4bgxenRE1","type":"tool_result","content":"## Part 5 — Exercise B: the token budget\n\n**Time: 45 min.** Graded as B7 (3 pts) and discussed in your report.\n\n### 5.1 Why you cannot budget in tokens\n\nAnthropic does not publish a token count for Pro. Usage is metered as a **rolling 5-hour session** plus a **weekly cap across all models**, and what you spend depends on message length, conversation length, attachments, tool use, model, and effort level. So you cannot plan in tokens. You can plan in **percent of your own meters**, and that is what this exercise builds.\n\n### 5.2 The meter\n\nPart 4's status line already reports rate limits when your plan provides them:\n\n```text\n[Sonnet·medium] ctx 52% ▓▓▓▓▓░░░░░ │ 5h 31% │ wk 18%\n```\n\n- **ctx** — how full this conversation is. Fix with `/compact` or `/clear`.\n- **5h** — your rolling session window. When it hits 100%, you wait.\n- **wk** — your weekly cap. This is the one that can end your project week on a Thursday.\n\n### 5.3 The ledger, which writes itself\n\nThe same `Stop` hook appends one row per turn to `notes/usage-ledger.csv`:\n\n```text\nts,session,phase,provider,model,effort,ctx_pct,five_h_pct,weekly_pct\n2026-09-30T14:03:11Z,7c1a9b02,T03,anthropic,Sonnet,medium,52.0,31,18\n```\n\nThe `phase` column comes from `.claude/state/phase`. `CLAUDE.md` tells Claude to write the phase there when a stage starts, so labeling is automatic. Check it yourself at any time:\n\n```bash\ncat .claude/state/phase        # expect: grill, spec, tickets, or T03\n```\n\n### 5.4 Write your budget plan (before your first ticket)\n\nCreate `notes/token-budget-plan.md`. Start from this default mapping, which follows Anthropic's own guidance — Sonnet for most coding, Opus for architecture and hard debugging, Haiku for quick mechanical work — and then **edit it to match what `/model` actually offers your account**:\n\n| Stage | Model | Effort | Your estimate (% of a 5-hour window) |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `/research`, quick lookups | Haiku | low | |\n| `/grill-with-docs`, `/to-spec`, `/to-tickets` | `opusplan` or Opus | high | |\n| `/implement` + `/tdd` (per ticket) | Sonnet | medium | |\n| `/code-review` (per ticket) | Sonnet | high | |\n\nThen answer, in writing: how many 5-hour windows will the 9 tickets take? How much of one week is that? Which stage will you cut first if you are wrong?\n\nYour first estimates will be bad. That is fine — 5.6 corrects them with data.\n\n### 5.5 Switch models deliberately\n\n```text\n/model              # see what your account has, and switch\n/model opusplan     # Opus while planning, Sonnet for execution\n```\n\n`/model opusplan` is built for exactly the split this workflow uses. Switching does not clear the conversation, so Sonnet still sees everything Opus decided.\n\nFour habits that stretch a window furthest, from Anthropic's own guidance: `/clear` between tasks; match the model to the job; reference files by path instead of pasting them; keep `CLAUDE.md` short, since it is prepended to every turn.\n\n### 5.6 Measure, then re-forecast\n\nThe template ships `scripts/usage_report.py` with one function left blank: `spend()`, which sums a meter's *increases* and ignores its resets. Four tests fail until you write it.\n\n```text\n/clear\n/tdd Implement spend() in scripts/usage_report.py so tests/test_usage_report.py passes. Do not change the tests.\n```\n\nAfter T01, and again after T03:\n\n```bash\nuv run python scripts/usage_report.py --remaining 6\n```\n\n**Expected:**\n\n```text\nphase          turns  5h spent   weekly  models / provider\nT01               14     22.0%     4.0%  Sonnet\ngrill             22     31.0%     6.0%  Opus\n\nTickets done: 1 · average weekly cost per ticket: 4.0%\nForecast: 6 tickets need ~24% of the weekly cap; 71% remains → FITS\n```\n\nIf the forecast says TIGHT or OVER BUDGET, apply the fixes in order: **lower effort → switch to Sonnet or Haiku → `/clear` more often → split fat tickets → Plan B (Part 9)**. Re-run after each change.\n\nYour report must compare plan against actual with real numbers from this ledger.\n\n---\n\n----\n# Token budget plan\n\n(Part 5.4 of the manual. Written before the first /implement.)\n\nDRAFT: the estimates below are first guesses. Edit them to match what `/model`\noffers on my account and what I expect to spend. The report will compare these\nagainst `notes/usage-ledger.csv`.\n\n## Model and effort per stage\n\n| Stage | Model | Effort | Estimate (% of a 5-hour window) |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `/research`, quick lookups | Haiku | low | 3% |\n| `/grill-with-docs`, `/to-spec`, `/to-tickets` | `opusplan` / Opus | high | 30% |\n| `/implement` + `/tdd` (per ticket) | Sonnet | medium | 12% each |\n| `/code-review` (per ticket) | Sonnet | high | 5% each |\n\n## Questions from the manual\n\n- **How many 5-hour windows will the 9 tickets take?** About 9 x 17% = 153% of a\n  window for the tickets, plus about 33% for research, grill, spec and tickets.\n  That is roughly 2 windows, spread over at least 2 days.\n- **How much of one week is that?** I have no per-window weekly cost yet. The first\n  ledger rows show about 3% of the weekly cap used after 11 turns of setup. After\n  T01 I will run `uv run python scripts/usage_report.py --remaining 8` and replace\n  this guess with a measured average.\n- **What gets cut first if I am wrong?** Extra-credit stretch goals first (they are\n  not needed to pass), then `/code-review` effort high -> medium, then I split any\n  ticket that pushes a session past 60% context.\n\n## Habits to stretch the window\n\n`/clear` between tickets, Sonnet for implementation, reference files by path\ninstead of pasting them, and keep `CLAUDE.md` short.\n----\ndiff --git a/scripts/usage_report.py b/scripts/usage_report.py\nindex 197b733..f0014f4 100644\n--- a/scripts/usage_report.py\n+++ b/scripts/usage_report.py\n@@ -33,7 +33,15 @@ def spend(series: list[float | None]) -> float:\n     >>> spend([None, 4.0, None, 7.0])\n     3.0\n     \"\"\"\n-    raise NotImplementedError(\"MP2 Exercise B: implement me with /tdd\")\n+    total = 0.0\n+    previous = None\n+    for value in series:\n+        if value is None:\n+            continue\n+        if previous is not None and value > previous:\n+            total += value - previous\n+        previous = value\n+    return total\n \n def read_rows(path: Path) -> list[dict]:\n     with path.open(newline=\"\") as f:","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"2091dfa7-cc4b-4a33-896a-db05154652b3","timestamp":"2026-10-13T00:57:08.285Z","toolUseResult":{"stdout":"## Part 5 — Exercise B: the token budget\n\n**Time: 45 min.** Graded as B7 (3 pts) and discussed in your report.\n\n### 5.1 Why you cannot budget in tokens\n\nAnthropic does not publish a token count for Pro. Switching does not clear the conversation, so Sonnet still sees everything Opus decided.\n\nFour habits that stretch a window furthest, from Anthropic's own guidance: `/clear` between tasks; match the model to the job; reference files by path instead of pasting them; keep `CLAUDE.md` short, since it is prepended to every turn.\n\n### 5.6 Measure, then re-forecast\n\nThe template ships `scripts/usage_report.py` with one function left blank: `spend()`, which sums a meter's *increases* and ignores its resets. Four tests fail until you write it.\n\n```text\n/clear\n/tdd Implement spend() in scripts/usage_report.py so tests/test_usage_report.py passes. Do not change the tests.\n```\n\nAfter T01, and again after T03:\n\n```bash\nuv run python scripts/usage_report.py --remaining 6\n```\n\n**Expected:**\n\n```text\nphase          turns  5h spent   weekly  models / provider\nT01               14     22.0%     4.0%  Sonnet\ngrill             22     31.0%     6.0%  Opus\n\nTickets done: 1 · average weekly cost per ticket: 4.0%\nForecast: 6 tickets need ~24% of the weekly cap; 71% remains → FITS\n```\n\nIf the forecast says TIGHT or OVER BUDGET, apply the fixes in order: **lower effort → switch to Sonnet or Haiku → `/clear` more often → split fat tickets → Plan B (Part 9)**. Re-run after each change.\n\nYour report must compare plan against actual with real numbers from this ledger.\n\n---\n\n----\n# Token budget plan\n\n(Part 5.4 of the manual. Written before the first /implement.)\n\nDRAFT: the estimates below are first guesses. Edit them to match what `/model`\noffers on my account and what I expect to spend. The report will compare these\nagainst `notes/usage-ledger.csv`.\n\n## Model and effort per stage\n\n| Stage | Model | Effort | Estimate (% of a 5-hour window) |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `/research`, quick lookups | Haiku | low | 3% |\n| `/grill-with-docs`, `/to-spec`, `/to-tickets` | `opusplan` / Opus | high | 30% |\n| `/implement` + `/tdd` (per ticket) | Sonnet | medium | 12% each |\n| `/code-review` (per ticket) | Sonnet | high | 5% each |\n\n## Questions from the manual\n\n- **How many 5-hour windows will the 9 tickets take?** About 9 x 17% = 153% of a\n  window for the tickets, plus about 33% for research, grill, spec and tickets.\n  That is roughly 2 windows, spread over at least 2 days.\n- **How much of one week is that?** I have no per-window weekly cost yet. The first\n  ledger rows show about 3% of the weekly cap used after 11 turns of setup. After\n  T01 I will run `uv run python scripts/usage_report.py --remaining 8` and replace\n  this guess with a measured average.\n- **What gets cut first if I am wrong?** Extra-credit stretch goals first (they are\n  not needed to pass), then `/code-review` effort high -> medium, then I split any\n  ticket that pushes a session past 60% context.\n\n## Habits to stretch the window\n\n`/clear` between tickets, Sonnet for implementation, reference files by path\ninstead of pasting them, and keep `CLAUDE.md` short.\n----\ndiff --git a/scripts/usage_report.py b/scripts/usage_report.py\nindex 197b733..f0014f4 100644\n--- a/scripts/usage_report.py\n+++ b/scripts/usage_report.py\n@@ -33,7 +33,15 @@ def spend(series: list[float | None]) -> float:\n     >>> spend([None, 4.0, None, 7.0])\n     3.0\n     \"\"\"\n-    raise NotImplementedError(\"MP2 Exercise B: implement me with /tdd\")\n+    total = 0.0\n+    previous = None\n+    for value in series:\n+        if value is None:\n+            continue\n+        if previous is not None and value > previous:\n+            total += value - {"parentUuid":"b6b67d36-ce6c-491a-a0c2-08a8c5f2032b","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"f6924c44-1e41-4b4d-aa10-da6238cc2f5e","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01SSzBKrcUD2bN1NZnMuwfhA","type":"tool_result","content":"grill\n## Part 6 — The loop\n\n**Time: 6–9 hours across several sittings. This is the project.**\n\n### 6.1 Grill (one session, fresh window)\n\n```text\n/clear\n/grill-with-docs I am building a small CMS for <your client, in one sentence>. Here are my field notes: notes/cms-field-notes.md and my brief: notes/client-brief.md. The 7 required capabilities are in the manual; help me decide what they mean for this client.\n```\n\nIt asks **one question at a time**, with a recommended answer attached. How to be good at this:\n\n- **Answer with your client's reality**, not with generalities. \"Karen posts weekly and will never remember a slug\" is worth more than \"yes, sounds good.\"\n- **Override the recommendation when you disagree**, and say why. That sentence is the single strongest evidence of your own judgment, and factor A samples exactly these answers.\n- **Watch `CONTEXT.md` fill up** as terms resolve. Open it in VS Code beside the terminal.\n- **Expect few or no ADRs.** They are reserved for decisions that are hard to reverse. Two are already written for you as examples.\n\nStop when you can answer, without hedging: what is a post here, what is a page, who may do what, what does publishing mean, and what is explicitly out of scope.\n\n### 6.2 Spec, then tickets (same unbroken window)\n\n```text\n/to-spec\n```\n\nIt turns the conversation into a spec and posts it as an issue labeled `spec`. Read the issue on GitHub. If it says something you did not decide, fix it now — everything downstream inherits it.\n\n```text\n/to-tickets\n```\n\n**Expected:** 7–9 issues labeled `ticket`, each with acceptance criteria and its blocking edges. Aim for tickets that look like this:\n\n- ✅ **T03 — Editors can create and edit posts** (touches the model, a route, a template, and tests; demoable alone)\n- ❌ **T03 — Add all database models** (one layer only; nothing works until everything lands)\n\nThis is the **tracer bullet** idea: each ticket is a narrow but complete path through every layer, verifiable the moment it lands. If a ticket cannot be demoed by itself, split or merge it now — `/to-tickets` again with what you want changed.\n\nCheck your set on GitHub:\n\n```bash\ngh issue list --label ticket\n```\n\n### 6.3 The per-ticket runbook\n\nRepeat for each ticket, in blocking order.
